#### 4.22 CL14-1 — Art. 14(1) — Crisis communication plans

**Source locus:** Art. 14(1).

> As part of the ICT risk management framework referred to in Article 6(1), financial entities shall have in place **crisis communication plans** enabling a **responsible disclosure** of, at least, **major ICT-related incidents or vulnerabilities** to clients and counterparts as well as to the public, as appropriate.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `responsible disclosure`**

`responsible` is Berry-flagged. Three readings collapsed to 2: R3 (entity-determined) is captured by R1 with entity latitude; collapsed to **R1 = timely + accurate** and **R2 = CVD-style (coordinated disclosure process)**.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Timely + accurate (R1 is literal; entity latitude) | In-Directive: literal `responsible` |
| R2 | CVD-style (vulnerability-disclosure-ethic-driven) | None in OJ |

R1 is the literal reading. R1 vs R2 admit materially distinct compliance populations (R2 admits a coordinated disclosure process; R1 admits ad-hoc statements). S3. `[no third reading identified]`.

*Berry anchor:* §3.3.1 (`responsible disclosure`).

**Instance 2 — POLY — S3 — `major ICT-related incidents or vulnerabilities` + `clients and counterparts as well as to the public` (3-way AND)**

Two disclosure-trigger categories (OR) + three audience categories (AND). Three readings collapsed to 2 per operator.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Major incidents only (no vulnerabilities) | None in OJ |
| R2 | Either (incidents OR vulnerabilities) | In-Directive: literal `incidents OR vulnerabilities` |
| R1' | Single audience (any one of three) | None in OJ |
| R2' | All three audiences (cumulative) | In-Directive: literal `clients AND counterparts AND public` |

R2 and R2' are the literal readings. S3 for both. `[no third reading identified]` for both — vulnerability-only (R3) is captured by R2.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (OR for trigger; AND for audience); §3.3.1.

**Definitional anchor:** DORA-D10 `major ICT-related incident` (S3); DORA-D16 `vulnerability` (NIS 2 Art. 6(15) cross-ref).
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies.

#### 4.23 CL14-2 — Art. 14(2)+(3) — Internal + external communication policies + designated person

`[scope: communication differentiation + designated spokesperson; multi-instance within one card per Q6=a convention]`

**Source locus:** Art. 14(2) and 14(3).

> As part of the ICT risk management framework, financial entities shall implement communication policies for internal staff and for external stakeholders. Communication policies for staff shall take into account the need to **differentiate between staff involved in ICT risk management**, in particular the staff responsible for response and recovery, and staff that needs to be informed.
>

**Instance 1 — POLY — S3 — `internal staff and external stakeholders` (Art. 14(2))**

Two audience categories (AND). Same pattern as CL11-2(4). Three readings collapsed to 2.

Two staff categories (POLY) + function descriptor. Three readings collapsed to 2 per operator. **Staff differentiation**: R1 (involved only or informed only) vs R2 (differentiate). **Public/media function**: R1 (spokesperson only) vs R2 (combined PR + media).

| R2' | Combined (PR + media) | In-Directive: literal `public AND media` |

R2 and R2' are the literal readings. R2 is S3; R2' is S2. `[no third reading identified]` for both — R3 (PR function only) is captured by R2'.

*Berry anchor:* §3.3.1 (`differentiate`, `public and media function`); §5.4.7 (AND).

#### 4.24 CL15-1 — Art. 15 chapeau + sub-points (a)–(g) — ESAs RTS mandate

`[scope: ESAs (Joint Committee + ENISA) → Commission; meta-procedural RTS mandate]`

**Source locus:** Art. 15 chapeau and sub-points (a)–(g).

> The ESAs shall, through the Joint Committee, in consultation with the European Union Agency on Cybersecurity (ENISA), develop common draft regulatory technical standards in order to:
>
> (a) **specify further elements to be included in the ICT security policies, procedures, protocols and tools** referred to in Article 9(2), with a view to ensuring the security of networks, enable adequate safeguards against intrusions and data misuse, preserve the availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data, including cryptographic techniques, and guarantee an accurate and prompt data transmission without major disruptions and undue delays;
>
> (b) develop further components of the controls of **access management rights referred to in Article 9(4), point (c)**, and associated human resource policy specifying access rights, procedures for granting and revoking rights, monitoring anomalous behaviour in relation to ICT risk through appropriate indicators, including for network use patterns, hours, IT activity and unknown devices;
>
> (c) develop further the mechanisms specified in **Article 10(1)** enabling a prompt detection of anomalous activities and the criteria set out in Article 10(2) triggering ICT-related incident detection and response processes;
>
> (d) specify further the components of the **ICT business continuity policy referred to in Article 11(1)**;
>
> (e) specify further the testing of ICT business continuity plans referred to in **Article 11(6)** to ensure that such testing duly takes into account scenarios in which the quality of the provision of a critical or important function deteriorates to an unacceptable level or fails, and duly considers the potential impact of the insolvency, or other failures, of any relevant ICT third-party service provider and, where relevant, the political risks in the respective providers' jurisdictions;
>
> (f) specify further the components of the **ICT response and recovery plans referred to in Article 11(3)**;
>
> (g) specifying further the content and format of the **report on the review of the ICT risk management framework** referred to in Article 6(5).
>
> When developing those draft regulatory technical standards, the ESAs shall take into account **the size and the overall risk profile** of the financial entity, and the nature, scale and complexity of its services, activities and operations, while duly taking into consideration any specific feature arising from the **distinct nature of activities across different financial services sectors**.
>
> The ESAs shall submit those draft regulatory technical standards to the Commission by **17 January 2024**.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `specify further` (verbatim from Art. 15 chapeau and sub-points) + `the size and the overall risk profile of the financial entity, and the nature, scale and complexity of its services, activities and operations` (RTS-calibration factors)**

The RTS specification mandate. The verbs vary across sub-points (`specify further elements`, `develop further components`, `further develop`, etc.) but the operational meaning is the same: ESAs fill in details. Three readings collapsed to 2.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Detailed technical specifications (comprehensive) | In-Directive: literal `specify further` |
| R2 | Light-touch specifications (references to standards) | None in OJ |
| R1' | Any single calibration factor (size, risk, N/S/C) | None in OJ |
| R2' | All four required (cumulative) | In-Directive: literal four-factor enumeration |

R1 and R2' are the literal readings. S3. `[no third reading identified]` — R3 (hybrid specifications) is captured by R1 with reference to standards.

*Berry anchor:* §3.3.1 (`specify further`, `nature, scale and complexity`); §5.4.7 (AND for factors); §5.5.

**Instance 2 — POLY — S3 — seven RTS subject areas (sub-points a–g) + sectoral calibration**

The 7 RTS subjects map to 7 Art. 9-11-6 elements. The RTS is multi-component. Three readings collapsed to 2.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Each RTS subject is independent (one RTS per subject) | None in OJ |
| R2 | Single joint RTS covering all seven subjects | In-Directive: literal `common draft regulatory technical standards` (joint) |

R2 is the literal reading. S3. `[no third reading identified]`.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (7-way coordination); §3.3.1 (`common`, `distinct nature`).

**Definitional anchor:** cross-ref to Art. 9(2), 9(4)(c), 10(1), 10(2), 11(1), 11(3), 11(6), 6(5).
**RTS-pending:** **YES.** Art. 15 RTS deadline 17 January 2024 (passed); as of mid-2025, **Delegated Regulation (EU) 2024/1774** of 31 May 2024 supplements DORA with the regulatory technical standards for Art. 15(a)–(g), adopted by the Commission on the basis of ESA Joint Committee draft. The adopted RTS is binding and resolves many S3 instances flagged above (e.g. specifics of Art. 9(2) security controls, Art. 11(6) BCP testing scenarios).
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 does not apply (clause is addressed to ESAs, not financial entities).

#### 4.25 CL16-1 — Art. 16(1) first sub-paragraph + (2) — Simplified framework scope + documentation/review

`[scope: Art. 5–15 exemption; addressed to small/exempted entities; multi-instance within one card per Q6=a convention]`

> Articles 5 to 15 of this Regulation shall not apply to **small and non-interconnected investment firms, payment institutions exempted pursuant to Directive (EU) 2015/2366**; institutions exempted pursuant to Directive 2013/36/EU in respect of which Member States have decided not to apply the option referred to in Article 2(4) of this Regulation; electronic money institutions exempted pursuant to Directive 2009/110/EC; and small institutions for occupational retirement provision.

It shall be **continuously improved on the basis of lessons derived from implementation and monitoring**. A report on the review of the ICT risk management framework shall be submitted to the competent authority upon its request.

#### 4.26 CL16-2 — Art. 16(1) second sub-paragraph (a)–(h) + (3) — Simplified obligations (parallel to Art. 9(4)) + ESA RTS

`[scope: simplified framework — small entities; multi-instance within one card per Q6=a convention; parallel to Art. 9(4) but at lower granularity; 8 sub-points (a)-(h) NOT split into 8 cards]`

**Source locus:** Art. 16(1) second sub-paragraph and sub-points (a)–(h) + Art. 16(3).

> Without prejudice to the first subparagraph, the entities listed in the first subparagraph shall:
>
> (a) put in place and maintain a **sound and documented ICT risk management framework** that details the mechanisms and measures aimed at a **quick, efficient and comprehensive management of ICT risk**, including for the protection of relevant physical components and infrastructures;
>
> (b) <mark>continuously monitor the security and functioning of all ICT systems</mark>;
>
> (c) <mark>minimise the impact of ICT risk</mark> through the use of sound, resilient and updated ICT systems, protocols and tools which are appropriate to support the performance of their activities and the provision of services and adequately protect availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data in the network and information systems;
>
> (d) allow <mark>sources of ICT risk and anomalies in the network and information systems to be promptly identified and detected and ICT-related incidents to be swiftly handled</mark>;
>
> (e) identify <mark>key dependencies on ICT third-party service providers</mark>;
>
> (f) ensure the continuity of critical or important functions, through <mark>business continuity plans and response and recovery measures, which include, at least, back-up and restoration measures</mark>;
>
> (g) <mark>test, on a regular basis, the plans and measures referred to in point (f), as well as the effectiveness of the controls</mark> implemented in accordance with points (a) and (c);
>
> (h) implement, as appropriate, relevant operational conclusions resulting from the tests referred to in point (g) and from post-incident analysis into the ICT risk assessment process and develop, according to needs and ICT risk profile, ICT security awareness programmes and digital operational resilience training for staff and management.
>
> 3. The ESAs shall, through the Joint Committee, in consultation with the ENISA, develop common draft regulatory technical standards in order to:
>
> (a) specify further the elements to be included in the ICT risk management framework referred to in paragraph 1, second subparagraph, point (a);
>
> (b) specify further the elements in relation to systems, protocols and tools to minimise the impact of ICT risk referred to in paragraph 1, second subparagraph, point (c), with a view to ensuring the security of networks, enabling adequate safeguards against intrusions and data misuse and preserving the availability, authenticity, integrity and confidentiality of data;
>
> (c) specify further the components of the ICT business continuity plans referred to in paragraph 1, second subparagraph, point (f);
>
> (d) specify further the rules on the testing of business continuity plans and ensure the effectiveness of the controls referred to in paragraph 1, second subparagraph, point (g) and ensure that such testing duly takes into account scenarios in which the quality of the provision of a critical or important function deteriorates to an unacceptable level or fails;
>
> (e) specify further the content and format of the report on the review of the ICT risk management framework referred to in paragraph 2.
>
> When developing those draft regulatory technical standards, the ESAs shall take into account **the size and the overall risk profile** of the financial entity, and the nature, scale and complexity of its services, activities and operations.
>
> The ESAs shall submit those draft regulatory technical standards to the Commission by **17 January 2024**.

**Instance 1 — COORD — S3 — chapeau: `quick, efficient and comprehensive management of ICT risk` (3-way AND) + sub-point (a): `put in place and maintain` (2-way AND) + `sound and documented ICT risk management framework`**

Three management qualifiers (AND) + two lifecycle phases + two framework qualifiers. Three readings collapsed to 2 per operator. **3-way**: R1 (composite) vs R2 (three discrete). **2-way put/maintain**: R1 (either) vs R2 (both). **Sound/documented**: R1 (either) vs R2 (both).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Three discrete metrics (for 3-way) | In-Directive: literal `quick, efficient AND comprehensive` |
| R2 | Composite (overall management quality) | None in OJ |
| R1' | Either put OR maintain (for 2-way) | None in OJ |
| R2' | Both required | In-Directive: literal |
| R1'' | Either sound OR documented (one suffices) | None in OJ |
| R2'' | Both qualifiers required | In-Directive: literal `sound AND documented` |

R1, R2', R2'' are the literal readings. S3. `[no third reading identified]` for all three operators.

*Berry anchor:* §5.5 (three-way coordination); §5.4.7 (AND); §5.1 (`sound`, `documented`).

Two temporal qualifiers + two backup measures. Three readings collapsed to 2 per operator. **Temporal**: R1 (either) vs R2 (both). **Backup**: R1 (one suffices) vs R2 (both).

Three test dimensions (AND) + three categories of follow-on. Three readings collapsed to 2 per operator. **Test**: R1 (each independent) vs R2 (all three required). **Follow-on**: R1 (each independent) vs R2 (all three required).

**Instance 5 — POLY — S3 — five RTS subject areas (sub-points a–e, Art. 16(3)) + `duly takes into account scenarios in which the quality of the provision of a critical or important function deteriorates to an unacceptable level or fails`**

Five RTS subjects + scenario test. Three readings collapsed to 2 per operator. **RTS**: R1 (each independent) vs R2 (joint). **Scenario**: R1 (only decline) vs R2 (decline or fail).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Each RTS subject is independent | None in OJ |
| R2 | Single joint RTS covering all five | In-Directive: literal `common draft regulatory technical standards` |
| R1' | Only decline scenarios (excludes failure) | None in OJ |
| R2' | Decline or failure (OR) | In-Directive: literal `deteriorates to an unacceptable level or fails` |

R2 and R2' are the literal readings. S3. `[no third reading identified]` for both.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (5-way coordination); §3.3.1 (`common`, `deteriorates to an unacceptable level`).

**Definitional anchor:** DORA Art. 3(2) → NIS 2 Art. 6(1); DORA-D22 `critical or important function` (S3); DORA Art. 3(19) `ICT third-party service provider`.
**RTS-pending:** Art. 16(3) mandates ESA RTS for the simplified framework (deadline 17 January 2024); as of mid-2025, **Reg. (EU) 2024/2956** (implementing) + Delegated Reg. (EU) 2024/1772 (delegated) jointly specify the simplified framework.
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 does not apply (clause IS the proportionality provision).

**Layer 1 observation — Art. 16 simplified framework is structurally parallel to Art. 9(4) hinge but at lower operational granularity.** The simplification is **substantive, not nominal**: the simplified framework omits access control (9(4)(c)), change management (9(4)(e)), and patches (9(4)(f)) as **standalone control families**. Some of these are absorbed into sub-points (a), (c), and (g) (e.g. data protection under (c) implies some security controls), but the **structural alignment is incomplete**. This is a deliberate regulatory choice: small entities are exempted from the most operationally demanding controls. **Layer 2 must adjudicate whether the simplification is proportional to the risk profile of exempted entities or whether it introduces a security gap.**

### 5. T3-vs-text gaps

This section catalogues **inconsistencies between the v0.1 T3 mapping** (T3 clauses **C14**, **C15**, **C16**, **C17**, **C26**, **C27**, **C28**) and the OJ text. Per pilot convention, T3-vs-text gaps are flagged separately from linguistic ambiguities of the regulation (they are inconsistencies of the mapping layer, not of the text). **Every gap below is a T3-imposed mapping artefact, not an OJ-text artefact.**

#### 5.1 T3 coverage check

| T3 clause | OJ locus | v0.2 mapping | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| **C14** | Art. 9(4)(c) | CL9-4 Instance 6 | **Major distortion** (§5.2.1). |
| **C15** | Art. 9(4)(a), (e), (f) | CL9-4 Instances 1, 10-15 | v0.2 restores single hinge card. |
| **C16** | Art. 9(4)(d) | CL9-4 Instance 8 | **Major distortion** (§5.2.2). |
| **C17** | Art. 9(4)(b), (c), (d) | CL9-4 Instances 2-9 | Single-hinge-card pattern. |
| **C26** | Art. 9(4)(f) [correct] / Art. 13(6) [mis-mapped] | CL9-4 Instances 14-15 + CL13-2 Instance 3 | **Mis-mapping** (§5.2.3). |
| **C27** | Art. 13(2) | CL13-1 Instance 2 | No major gap. |
| **C28** | Art. 13(6) | CL13-2 Instance 3 | **Major distortion** (§5.2.4). |
| **— (NEW)** | Art. 7-12, 14, 15, 16 | CL7-1 … CL16-2 | T3 omitted 19 clauses; v0.2 adds them. |

#### 5.2 Major T3 distortion gaps (with OJ-text rebuttals)

## DORA v0.2 — Chapter III: ICT-Related Incident Management, Classification, and Reporting (Art. 17–23)

> **Source.** OJ L 333, 27.12.2022, p. 39–44. `03_REFERENCE_MATERIAL/Regulatory_References/DORA.txt`, lines **2275–2591**.
>
> **Pattern.** Each article quoted in full (paragraph by paragraph), then per-clause analysis. Universal `[recital NN]` convention. DORA-specific fields: `definitional-anchor`, `proportionality-status: Art. 4 applies`, `RTS-pending: Art. 18(3) | Art. 20` (the defining characteristic of Ch. III — hard deadlines and classification thresholds live in RTS, not OJ), `scope: regulator→financial-entity` (for client-notification and CA-routing clauses).
>
> **Chapter III is the operational reporting arm of DORA.** It operationalises the Art. 3(8) `ICT-related incident` and Art. 3(10) `major ICT-related incident` definitions. Art. 17 establishes the management process. Art. 18 enumerates 6 multiplicative criteria for classifying incidents (and 3 for cyber threats) and delegates the **combination rule + materiality thresholds** to an ESA RTS (Art. 18(3)). Art. 19 mandates reporting of major ICT-related incidents in 3 tiers (initial / intermediate / final), plus voluntary cyber-threat notification and mandatory client notification. Art. 20 is a pure RTS/ITS delegation — content, time limits, and templates are **wholly dependent** on the ESAs Joint Committee output (deadline 17 July 2024). Art. 21 is a feasibility-report mandate on centralisation (a single EU Hub). Art. 22 specifies supervisory feedback. Art. 23 extends Ch. III to operational/security payment-related incidents for credit institutions, payment institutions, AISPs, and EMIs.
>
> **Articles in this file:** 7 articles, ~30 paragraphs, **31 atomic clauses** (CL17-1 … CL23-1).

---

### 1. Articles covered

| Article | Title (OJ) | Paragraphs | New in v0.2? | T3 clause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Art. 17 | **ICT-related incident management process** | 3 (1, 2, 3 with 6 sub-points (a)–(f)) | No (extended) | **C29** |
| Art. 18 | **Classification of ICT-related incidents and cyber threats** | 4 (1 with 6 sub-points (a)–(f), 2, 3 with 3 sub-points (a)–(c), 4) | No (extended) | **C30** |
| Art. 19 | **Reporting of major ICT-related incidents and voluntary notification of significant cyber threats** | 8 (1, 2, 3, 4 with 3 sub-points (a)–(c), 5, 6 with 5 sub-points (a)–(e), 7, 8) | No (extended) | **C31** |
| Art. 20 | **Harmonisation of reporting content and templates** | 1 (RTS+ITS) | No (extended) | — (RTS meta — T3 omitted) |
| Art. 21 | **Centralisation of reporting of major ICT-related incidents** (single EU Hub feasibility) | 3 (1, 2 with 7 sub-points (a)–(g), 3) | **YES** | — (not in T3) |
| Art. 22 | **Supervisory feedback** | 2 (1, 2) | **YES** | — (not in T3) |
| Art. 23 | **Operational or security payment-related incidents concerning credit institutions, payment institutions, account information service providers, and electronic money institutions** (extension of Ch. III to payment-sector entities) | 1 | **YES** | — (not in T3) |

**Note on Art. 4 (Proportionality):** every clause card in this file carries `proportionality-status: Art. 4 applies`. Art. 18(4) explicitly names microenterprises and SMEs.

**Note on Art. 20 (RTS-pending — THE defining characteristic of Ch. III):** Ch. III is structurally dependent on RTS/ITS acts. The hard reporting deadlines and classification thresholds do not live in OJ text — they are mandated to the ESAs Joint Committee. As of mid-2025:

| RTS/ITS | Mandate locus | OJ deadline | Status (mid-2025) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Art. 18(3) RTS (criteria + materiality thresholds) | Art. 18(3)(a)–(c) | **17 January 2024** | Deadline **passed**; ESAs Joint Committee RTS in adoption pipeline. Implementing Regulation (EU) 2024/2956 of 18 July 2024 partially addresses content. |
| Art. 20 RTS (content + time limits) | Art. 20(a)(i)–(iii) | **17 July 2024** | Deadline **passed**; partly addressed by Reg. (EU) 2024/2956. |
| Art. 20 ITS (forms, templates, procedures) | Art. 20(b) | **17 July 2024** | **Adopted** as Implementing Regulation (EU) 2024/2956 (Annex I–IV templates). |
| Art. 21(3) joint report (single EU Hub) | Art. 21(1)–(3) | **17 January 2025** | Deadline **passed**; ESAs Joint Committee report published in Q1 2025. |

**Note on definitional anchors:** every ICT-related clause in Ch. III inherits from DORA Art. 3(2) → NIS 2 Art. 6(1) (`network and information system`). Major-incident classification inherits from Art. 3(10) `major ICT-related incident` (DORA-D10, S3 regress — see pilot file §2.4), which inherits from Art. 3(22) `critical or important function` (DORA-D22 tautology, S3).

**Note on v0.2 expansion:** T3 (T4 taxonomy) covered Ch. III as 3 clauses (C29, C30, C31) corresponding to Arts 17, 18, 19 in compressed form. v0.2 expands this to 31 atomic clauses and **adds 4 articles** (Art. 20, 21, 22, 23) that T3 entirely omitted (T3 dropped them as "procedural" or "payment-specific").

### 3. Verbatim OJ text — Art. 17–23

#### 4.1 CL17-1 — Art. 17(1) — Process establishment

**Source locus:** Art. 17(1).

> Financial entities shall **define, establish and implement** an ICT-related incident management process to detect, manage and notify ICT-related incidents.

**Instance 1 — COORD — S2 — `define, establish and implement`**

Three coordinated verbs; the standard for each is undefined. Same pattern as DORA-D01 `build, assure and review` and Art. 24(1) `establish, maintain and review` (CL24-1 in Ch. IV file).

- **R1:** Three discrete lifecycle phases (define-then-establish-then-implement).
- **R2:** Three aspects of a single continuous process.
- **R3:** Three independent control families (define policy, establish system, implement operationally).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Discrete phases | In-Directive: literal three-verb coordination |
| R2 | Continuous | None in OJ |
| R3 | Independent families | None in OJ |

R1 vs R3 admit materially distinct compliance populations. S2.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (three-way coordination).

**Instance 2 — COORD — S2 — `detect, manage and notify`**

Three coordinated purposes of the process. Same Berry pattern.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (three-way coordination).

**Definitional anchor:** DORA Art. 3(2) → NIS 2 Art. 6(1) `network and information system`; DORA Art. 3(8) `ICT-related incident`.
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies.

---

#### 4.26 CL20-1 — Art. 20 — ESAs RTS/ITS mandate (whole article)

**Source locus:** Art. 20, in its entirety.

> The ESAs, through the Joint Committee, and in consultation with ENISA and the ECB, shall develop:
>
> (a) common draft regulatory technical standards in order to:
>
> (i) establish the content of the reports for major ICT-related incidents in order to reflect the criteria laid down in Article 18(1) and incorporate further elements, such as details for establishing the relevance of the reporting for other Member States and whether it constitutes a major operational or security payment-related incident or not;
>
> (ii) determine the time limits for the initial notification and for each report referred to in Article 19(4);
>
> (iii) establish the content of the notification for significant cyber threats.
>
> When developing those draft regulatory technical standards, the ESAs shall take into account the **size and the overall risk profile of the financial entity, and the nature, scale and complexity of its services, activities and operations**, and in particular, with a view to ensuring that, for the purposes of this paragraph, point (a), point (ii), different time limits may reflect, as appropriate, specificities of financial sectors, without prejudice to maintaining a consistent approach to ICT-related incident reporting pursuant to this Regulation and to Directive (EU) 2022/2555. The ESAs shall, as applicable, provide justification when deviating from the approaches taken in the context of that Directive;
>
> (b) common draft implementing technical standards in order to establish the standard forms, templates and procedures for financial entities to report a major ICT-related incident and to notify a significant cyber threat.
>
> The ESAs shall submit the common draft regulatory technical standards referred to in the first paragraph, point (a), and the common draft implementing technical standards referred to in the first paragraph, point (b), to the Commission by **17 July 2024**.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `size and the overall risk profile of the financial entity, and the nature, scale and complexity of its services, activities and operations`**

A four-factor proportionality formulation (size + risk profile + nature + scale + complexity, 5 adjectives actually). Same Berry pattern as Art. 4(2) chapeau. S3 (each factor admits S2 reading; the combination rule is itself undefined).

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (5-way AND); §3.3.1 (`size`, `risk profile`, `nature, scale and complexity`); cf. DORA Art. 4(2).

**Instance 2 — VAG — S3 — `specificities of financial sectors`**

`specificities` undefined. Three readings:

- **R1:** Sectoral (banking vs. insurance vs. securities).
- **R2:** Sub-sectoral (retail banking vs. investment banking).
- **R3:** Business-model-specific.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Sectoral | None in OJ |
| R2 | Sub-sectoral | In-Directive: recital 56 |
| R3 | Business-model | None in OJ |

S3.

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`specificities`); §3.3.1 (`financial sectors`).

**Instance 3 — VAG — S3 — `consistent approach … pursuant to this Regulation and to Directive (EU) 2022/2555`**

`consistent approach` between DORA reporting and NIS 2 reporting (NIS 2 Art. 23; cf. NIS 2 file §3.10). S3.

*Berry anchor:* §3.3.1 (`consistent approach`).

**Instance 4 — POLY — S2 — `standard forms, templates and procedures`**

Three operational artefacts (AND). S2.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (AND); §3.3.1 (`forms`, `templates`, `procedures`).

**Definitional anchor:** DORA-D10; DORA-D13.
**RTS-pending:** **YES — Art. 20 is the pure RTS+ITS delegation of Ch. III.** Both deadlines = 17 July 2024.

| Sub-act | Status (mid-2025) |
|---|---|
| RTS under (a)(i) (report content) | **Adopted** as Implementing Regulation (EU) 2024/2956 Annex II–IV. |
| RTS under (a)(ii) (time limits) | Per-sector time limits laid down in Reg. (EU) 2024/2956 Annex II. |
| RTS under (a)(iii) (cyber-threat notification content) | **Adopted** in Reg. (EU) 2024/2956 Annex III. |
| ITS under (b) (standard forms, templates, procedures) | **Adopted** in Reg. (EU) 2024/2956 Annex IV. |

Net: as of mid-2025, **the Art. 20 mandates are substantially implemented via Reg. (EU) 2024/2956** but Layer 1 catalogues the RTS-pending character because OJ Art. 20 is, by its own terms, an RTS delegation.

**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies (explicit cross-reference).

#### 4.27 CL21-1 — Art. 21(1)+(2) — Single EU Hub feasibility mandate

**Source locus:** Art. 21(1) and 21(2).

> 1. The ESAs, through the Joint Committee, and in consultation with the ECB and ENISA, shall prepare a joint report assessing the **feasibility of further centralisation of incident reporting through the establishment of a single EU Hub for major ICT-related incident reporting** by financial entities. The joint report shall explore ways to facilitate the flow of ICT-related incident reporting, reduce associated costs and underpin thematic analyses with a view to enhancing supervisory convergence.
>
> 2. The joint report referred to in paragraph 1 shall comprise at least the following elements:
>
> (a) prerequisites for the establishment of a single EU Hub;
>
> (b) benefits, limitations and risks, including risks associated with the high concentration of sensitive information;
>
> (c) the necessary capability to ensure interoperability with regard to other relevant reporting schemes;
>
> (d) elements of operational management;
>
> (e) conditions of membership;
>
> (f) technical arrangements for financial entities and national competent authorities to access the single EU Hub;
>
> (g) a preliminary assessment of financial costs incurred by setting-up the operational platform supporting the single EU Hub, including the requisite expertise.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `single EU Hub`**

`single` + `Hub` — the architectural choice is undefined.

- **R1:** Single centralised data platform (one location, all CAs access).
- **R2:** Single API gateway (each CA holds data, common API).
- **R3:** Single standard (distributed but interoperable).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Centralised platform | In-Directive: literal `single EU Hub` |
| R2 | API gateway | None in OJ |
| R3 | Single standard | None in OJ |

S3.

*Berry anchor:* §3.3.1 (`single`, `Hub`); §5.1 (`single`).

**Instance 2 — VAG — S3 — `feasibility`**

`feasibility` of centralisation is broad — technical + legal + operational + financial + governance. S3.

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`feasibility`).

**Instance 3 — VAG — S3 — `thematic analyses with a view to enhancing supervisory convergence`**

`thematic analyses` + `supervisory convergence` — undefined. S3.

*Berry anchor:* §3.3.1 (`thematic analyses`, `supervisory convergence`).

**Instance 4 — VAG — S2 — `high concentration of sensitive information`**

`high concentration` is undefined. Same Berry pattern as DORA-D29 `ICT concentration risk`. S2.

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`high concentration`); cf. DORA-D29.

**Instance 5 — POLY — S2 — 7 enumerated elements**

Each element admits its own internal ambiguity:

| Element | Key ambiguity | Berry anchor |
|---|---|---|
| (a) `prerequisites` | Hard vs. soft preconditions | §3.3.1 (`prerequisites`) |
| (b) `benefits, limitations and risks` | Three-way comparison standard | §5.4.7 (three-way) |
| (c) `interoperability` | Technical vs. semantic | §3.3.1 (`interoperability`) |
| (d) `operational management` | Undefined scope | §3.3.1 (`operational management`) |
| (e) `conditions of membership` | Who becomes a member? | §3.3.1 (`membership`) |
| (f) `technical arrangements` | Read-only vs. write access | §3.3.1 (`technical arrangements`) |
| (g) `preliminary assessment of financial costs` | Method-based vs. estimate-based | §3.3.1 (`preliminary`) |

S2 (per-element); aggregate S3 due to the open-ended `at least` qualifier.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.5 (illustrative use — `at least`); §3.3.1.

**Definitional anchor:** DORA-D29 `ICT concentration risk`; DORA Art. 50 (administrative penalties context).
**Scope:** regulator-internal (between ESAs and NCA institutional architecture, not directly entity-facing).
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies.

#### 4.31 CL23-1 — Art. 23 — Ch. III extension to operational/security payment-related incidents

**Source locus:** Art. 23 (single paragraph).

> The requirements laid down in this Chapter shall also apply to operational or security payment-related incidents and to major operational or security payment-related incidents, **where they concern credit institutions, payment institutions, account information service providers, and electronic money institutions**.

**Instance 1 — POLY — S3 — `operational OR security payment-related`**

Two payment-incident dimensions (OR).

- **R1:** Operational incidents (system availability, processing errors).
- **R2:** Security incidents (fraud, breach, unauthorised access).
- **R3:** Either.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Operational | In-Directive: literal |
| R2 | Security | In-Directive: literal |
| R3 | Either | In-Directive: literal `or` |

R3 is the literal reading. S3.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (OR); §3.3.1 (`operational`, `security`, `payment-related`).

**Instance 2 — POLY — S3 — 4 payment-entity categories**

`credit institutions` + `payment institutions` + `account information service providers` + `electronic money institutions` — four categories coordinated by commas (AND). Each entity type inherits a cross-referenced definition (see below). S3 (the combination rule is associative; in the OJ, all four categories are subject to Ch. III equally).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | All four (AND) | In-Directive: literal |
| R2 | Each separately | None in OJ |

R1 is the literal reading. S3.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (AND); §3.3.1 (`credit institutions`, `payment institutions`, `AISPs`, `EMIs`).

**Definitional anchor (compound — each entity inherits from its sectoral regulation):**

| Entity | DORA Art. 3 cross-ref | Source regulation |
|---|---|---|
| `credit institution` | (31) | CRR Reg. 575/2013 Art. 4(1)(1) |
| `payment institution` | (35) | PSD2 Dir. 2015/2366 Art. 4(4) |
| `account information service provider` | (37) | PSD2 Dir. 2015/2366 Art. 33(1) |
| `electronic money institution` | (38) | EMD Dir. 2009/110/EC Art. 2(1) |

**Definitional anchor (incident cross-references):** DORA Art. 3(9) `operational or security payment-related incident` (DORA-D09); DORA Art. 3(11) `major operational or security payment-related incident` (DORA-D11 — parallels DORA-D10 S3 for the payment sector).

**Definitional anchor (definition cross-references in Art. 18):** Art. 18(3)(a) explicitly applies the materiality-threshold RTS to **major operational or security payment-related incidents** as well as major ICT-related incidents — i.e. the same Art. 18(3) RTS covers both.

**Scope:** sectoral expansion of Ch. III — same obligations, different entity classes.
**RTS-pending:** Art. 18(3)(a) (for the `major operational or security payment-related incident` threshold).
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies (the Art. 18(4) proportionality microenterprise consideration flows through).

---

### 5. T3-vs-text gaps

Per pilot convention, T3-vs-text gaps are flagged separately from linguistic ambiguities of the regulation (they are inconsistencies of the mapping layer, not of the text).

#### 6.7 Art. 23 — payment-sector extension (PSD2+EMD coverage)

Art. 23 **extends the entire Ch. III** to operational or security payment-related incidents for **4 payment-sector entity types**: credit institutions, payment institutions (PSDs Art. 4(4)), account information service providers (PSD2 Art. 33(1)), electronic money institutions (EMD Art. 2(1)).

- **Berry anchor:** §5.4.7 (AND/OR across 4 entity categories); §3.3.1 (`payment-related`).
- **Structural implication:** the **payment-sector reporting obligations are identical** to the ICT-related reporting obligations (same 3-tier structure, same CA routing). The only differentiation is the **incident type** (`operational or security payment-related` instead of `ICT-related`) and the **classification thresholds** (per Art. 18(3)(a) RTS — applies to `major operational or security payment-related incidents` alongside major ICT-related incidents).
- **Cross-regulation consistency:** Art. 23 is the **DORA↔PSD2 integration point**. Prior to DORA, payment-related incidents were reported under PSD2 Art. 96(1) with sectoral variations; DORA Art. 23 supersedes the PSD2 reporting architecture for the four entity types above.
- **Layer 1 catalogue only.** Layer 2 must surface the **PSD2 derogation analysis** (which PSD2 reporting obligations are preserved vs. replaced).

## DORA v0.2 — Chapter IV: Digital Operational Resilience Testing (Art. 24–27)

> **Source.** OJ L 333, 27.12.2022, p. 45–48. `03_REFERENCE_MATERIAL/Regulatory_References/DORA.txt`, lines **2595–2850**.
>
> **Pattern.** Each article quoted in full, then per-clause analysis. Universal `[recital NN]` convention. DORA-specific fields: `definitional-anchor`, `proportionality-status: Art. 4 applies`, `RTS-pending: Art. 20` (where applicable), `definitional-anchor: DORA Art. 26(11) → TIBER-EU` (non-binding guidance).
>
> **Chapter IV establishes the testing programme.** Art. 24 sets the general programme as an integral part of the Art. 6 ICT risk-management framework. Art. 25 enumerates (in an open list) the test modalities. Art. 26 mandates Threat-Led Penetration Testing (TLPT) on a hard 3-year cadence, anchored to the **TIBER-EU** framework (non-binding). Art. 27 specifies requirements for testers (NEW in v0.2; T3 omitted Art. 27 entirely).
>
> **Articles in this file:** 4 articles, 24 paragraphs, 17 atomic clauses (CL24-1 … CL27-3).

---

### 1. Articles covered

| Article | Title | Paragraphs | New in v0.2? | T3 clause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Art. 24 | General requirements for the performance of digital operational resilience testing | 6 | No (extended) | **C32** |
| Art. 25 | Testing of ICT tools and systems | 3 | No (extended) | **C33** |
| Art. 26 | Advanced testing of ICT tools, systems and processes based on TLPT | 11 | No (extended) | **C34** |
| Art. 27 | Requirements for testers for the carrying out of TLPT | 3 | **YES** | — (not in T3) |

**Note on Art. 4 (Proportionality):** every clause card in this file carries `proportionality-status: Art. 4 applies` (per pilot convention).

**Note on Art. 20 (RTS-pending):** no RTS mandate in Ch. IV specifically (the RTS mandate is in Art. 26(11) for TLPT details, addressed inline).

**Note on definitional anchors:** every ICT-related clause in Ch. IV inherits from DORA Art. 3(2) → NIS 2 Art. 6(1) (`network and information system`). The TLPT definitional anchor is DORA Art. 26(11) → **TIBER-EU** (non-binding ECB framework) — same **binding-to-non-binding definitional regress** pattern as CRA Annex I → ISO standards.

### 3. Verbatim OJ text — Art. 24–27

#### 4.1 CL24-1 — Art. 24(1)+(2) chapeau — Programme establishment

**Source locus:** Art. 24(1) and 24(2).

> For the purpose of assessing preparedness for handling ICT-related incidents, of identifying weaknesses, deficiencies and gaps in digital operational resilience, and of promptly implementing corrective measures, financial entities, other than microenterprises, shall, taking into account the criteria set out in Article 4(2), **establish, maintain and review** a **sound and comprehensive digital operational resilience testing programme** as an integral part of the ICT risk-management framework referred to in Article 6.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `sound and comprehensive`**

The qualifier `sound and comprehensive` is undefined.

- **R1:** Strict reading: tests must cover every component and every modality (exhaustive).
- **R2:** Risk-proportional reading: tests must cover enough of the system to detect material weaknesses.
- **R3:** Methodology-based reading: tests must apply an industry-recognised methodology (e.g. NIST SP 800-115, OWASP WSTG, TIBER-EU).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Exhaustive | None in OJ |
| R2 | Risk-proportional | In-Directive: Art. 4(2) proportionality |
| R3 | Methodology-based | None in OJ |

R1 vs R3 admit materially distinct compliance populations (exhaustive vs. methodology-defined). S3.

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`sound`, `comprehensive`).

**Instance 2 — COORD — S2 — `establish, maintain and review`**

Three coordinated verbs. Same pattern as DORA-D01 `build, assure and review` (see pilot file §2.1). S2.

*Berry anchor:* §5.4.7 (three-way coordination); cf. pilot §2.1.

**Instance 3 — POLY — S2 — `promptly`**

`promptly implementing corrective measures` — same Berry-flagged vague temporal term as GDPR Art. 33 and CRA Art. 14 (C25).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Immediately upon test completion | In-Directive: literal `promptly` |
| R2 | Within a reasonable period | None in OJ |
| R3 | Per entity-defined SLA | None in OJ |

S2 (each reading yields a different remediation SLA, but the obligation is to remediate regardless).

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`promptly`).

**Definitional anchor:** DORA Art. 3(2) → NIS 2 Art. 6(1) `network and information system`.
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies (proportionality recurs 3 times in Art. 24(1), (3), (6)).

#### 4.2 CL24-2 — Art. 24(3) — Risk-based approach

**Source locus:** Art. 24(3).

> financial entities, other than microenterprises, shall follow a **risk-based approach** taking into account the criteria set out in Article 4(2) duly considering **the evolving landscape of ICT risk, any specific risks to which the financial entity concerned is or might be exposed, the criticality of information assets and of services provided, as well as any other factor the financial entity deems appropriate**.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `any other factor the financial entity deems appropriate`**

The catch-all clause. The entity is its own judge of what counts.

- **R1:** Entity-defined additional factors (broad entity discretion).
- **R2:** Factors consistent with Art. 4(2) criteria.
- **R3:** Factors listed in ESA guidance or national guidance.

| R1 | Entity discretion | In-Directive: literal `the financial entity deems appropriate` |
| R2 | Art. 4(2)-aligned | In-Directive: Art. 4(2) criteria |
| R3 | ESA/national guidance | None in OJ |

R1 is the literal reading. R1 vs R3 admit materially distinct compliance populations. S3.

**Instance 2 — POLY — S2 — `the evolving landscape of ICT risk`**

`evolving` admits three readings:
- **R1:** Time-bound (this calendar year).
- **R2:** Open-ended (continuously evolving).
- **R3:** Threat-intelligence-driven (per current threat intel feeds).

|---|---|---|
| R1 | Time-bound | None in OJ |

#### 4.9 CL26-1 — Art. 26(1) — TLPT cadence

**Source locus:** Art. 26(1).

> Financial entities, other than entities referred to in Article 16(1), first subparagraph, and other than microenterprises, which are identified in accordance with paragraph 8, third subparagraph, of this Article, shall carry out **at least every 3 years** advanced testing by means of TLPT. Based on the risk profile of the financial entity and taking into account operational circumstances, the competent authority may, where necessary, request the financial entity to **reduce or increase this frequency**.

**Instance 1 — POLY — S2 — `at least every 3 years`**

`at least every 3 years` admits three readings:
- **R1:** The gap between two consecutive TLPTs must be ≥3 years.
- **R2:** TLPTs must occur in years divisible by 3 (calendar 3-year cycle).
- **R3:** Three complete TLPTs per decade (rolling average).

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Gap ≥3 years | In-Directive: literal `at least every 3 years` |
| R2 | Calendar 3-year cycle | None in OJ |
| R3 | Rolling average | None in OJ |

R1 is the literal reading. S2 (the operational practice converges on R1).

*Berry anchor:* §5.7 (temporal operator); §5.1 (`at least`).

**Instance 2 — VAG — S3 — `where necessary, request the financial entity to reduce or increase this frequency`**

The trigger and bounds are undefined.

- **R1:** CA may reduce frequency based on low risk profile.
- **R2:** CA may increase frequency based on high risk profile.
- **R3:** Both.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Reduce only | In-Directive: literal `reduce or increase` |
| R2 | Increase only | In-Directive: literal `reduce or increase` |
| R3 | Either | In-Directive: literal |

R3 is the literal reading. The `where necessary` hedge does not specify what `necessary` means (high-risk evolution? significant findings from prior TLPT? sectoral threat?). S3.

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`where necessary`); §5.4.7 (OR in discretion).

**Definitional anchor:** DORA Art. 3(17) `TLPT` (DORA-D17) → Art. 26(11) → TIBER-EU.
**RTS-pending:** Art. 26(11) (see CL26-6).
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies.

#### 4.10 CL26-2 — Art. 26(2) — TLPT scope

**Source locus:** Art. 26(2).

**Instance 1 — POLY — S2 — `several or all critical or important functions`**

`several` vs. `all`:
- **R1:** Several (≥2) suffices; all is not required.
- **R2:** All is the default; several only when CA approves.
- **R3:** Either, per CA determination.

**Instance 3 — POLY — S2 — `underlying ICT systems, processes and technologies`**

Three categories (AND). Distinct scopes:

- **R2:** Compound: any combination of the three.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |

#### 4.15 CL27-1 — Art. 27(1)(a)–(e) — Tester requirements (external)

**Source locus:** Art. 27(1), five sub-points.

> Financial entities shall only use testers for the carrying out of TLPT, that:

> (c) **are certified by an accreditation body in a Member State** or **adhere to formal codes of conduct or ethical frameworks**;
>
> (d) provide an **independent assurance, or an audit report**, in relation to the sound management of risks associated with the carrying out of TLPT, including the **due protection of the financial entity's confidential information and redress for the business risks of the financial entity**;
>

#### 4.16 CL27-2 — Art. 27(2)(a)–(c) — Internal-tester additional conditions

**Source locus:** Art. 27(2).

> When using internal testers, financial entities shall ensure that, in addition to the requirements in paragraph 1, the following conditions are met:

**Instance 1 — VAG — S3 — `approved by the relevant competent authority or by the single public authority` (sub-point a)**

`approved` admits three readings:

#### 6.2 Art. 26(1) — Hard numeric anchor (3 years) reduces temporal ambiguity

The `at least every 3 years` cadence is a **hard numeric anchor**. This is the same Berry-relief pattern as CRA Annex I §2(c) `automatic security updates installed within an appropriate timeframe` (no anchor) vs. CRA Annex II §4 `disseminated without delay` (no anchor).

- **Berry anchor:** §5.7 (temporal operators); §5.1 (`at least every 3 years`).
- **Structural implication:** the 3-year cadence materially reduces temporal ambiguity for TLPT (compared to the year-by-year discretionary `at least yearly` of Art. 24(6), which is S2). However, the CA discretion clause (`reduce or increase this frequency`) partially reopens the ambiguity (CL26-1 Instance 2, S3).
- **Layer 1 observation:** hard numeric anchors are **the single most effective disambiguation technique** in EU cybersecurity regulations. DORA uses two of them in Ch. IV: 3 years (Art. 26(1)) and yearly (Art. 24(6)). Both anchor the temporal operator; the 3-year anchor is more rigid and less vulnerable to entity discretion.

#### 6.3 Art. 27(1)–(2) — Tester independence (POLY/S3 + VAG/S3)

Art. 27 specifies five external-tester requirements (sub-points (a)–(e)) and three internal-tester additional conditions (sub-points (a)–(c)). The pattern is **stricter than Art. 24(4)** (which requires independence without specifying mechanism) and is the **only place in Ch. IV** where the regulation goes into operational detail on WHO performs the test.

- **Berry anchor:** §3.3.1 (independence); §5.1 (`highest suitability`); §3.3.1 (`professional indemnity`).
- **Structural implication:** Art. 27 creates a **two-track tester regime** — external (5 requirements) vs. internal (5 + 3 additional). The internal track is stricter on conflicts-of-interest avoidance (Art. 27(2)(b)) but allows reuse of the financial entity's own threat-intel resources, partially offset by the requirement that the **threat-intelligence provider** be external (Art. 27(2)(c)).
- **Cross-regulation consistency:** CRA Annex I Part II §3 requires `effective and regular tests and reviews` without specifying tester qualifications. NIS 2 Art. 21(2)(e) requires `policies and procedures … to assess the effectiveness of cybersecurity risk-management measures` without specifying tester qualifications. **DORA Art. 27 is the most specific tester-qualification regime in the 5-regulation corpus.** This is justified by the high-stakes nature of TLPT (live production systems, systemic risk).

#### 4.9 CL30-1 — Art. 30(1) — Written contract + format

**Source locus:** Art. 30(1).

> The rights and obligations of the financial entity and of the ICT third-party service provider shall be **clearly allocated and set out in writing** …. include the service level agreements and be documented in **one written document** …. available to the parties **on paper, or in a document with another downloadable, durable and accessible format**.

**Instance 1 — VAG — S2 — `clearly allocated` + `one written document` + `downloadable, durable and accessible format` (consolidated)**

Three Berry-classic qualifiers in the chapeau. `clearly` undefined; `one written document` admits multiple readings; `downloadable, durable and accessible` is a three-property AND list. Three readings of the format-specification gate:

- **R1:** Explicit bilateral commitments in a single PDF; all three format properties required.
- **R2:** Sufficient detail to enable enforcement in a master agreement + incorporated documents; CA-determined format.

| # | Reading | Disambiguation source |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | Single PDF + all three properties | In-Directive: literal `one written document` + `and` |
| R2 | Master + incorporated + CA-determined | None in OJ |

R1 is the literal reading; R1 vs R2 admit materially distinct compliance populations. S2.

*Berry anchor:* §5.1 (`clearly`); §3.3.1 (`one written document`); §5.4.7 (AND).

**Definitional anchor:** DORA Art. 3(21) DORA-D21 `ICT services`.
**Proportionality-status:** Art. 4 applies.
**RTS-pending:** No (baseline obligation).
**Scope:** `regulator→financial-entity` + propagated to CTPP contracts.

## AI_Act

#### 2.12 AIA-C12 — Art. 13(1) Transparency

**Source locus:** `High-risk AI systems shall be designed and developed in such a way as to ensure that their operation is sufficiently transparent to enable deployers to interpret a system's output and use it appropriately. An appropriate type and degree of transparency shall be ensured`.

* **VAG — S3** — `sufficiently transparent`, `appropriate type and degree of transparency`. Two vague anchors in one sentence, both modifying the same concept (transparency).
* **POLY — S3** — `transparent` — three distinct AI-governance senses: (i) explainability (the deployer can understand why the system produced this output); (ii) disclosure (the deployer is told what the system does in general); (iii) interpretability (the deployer can map inputs to outputs). The three senses imply different technical implementations (XAI tooling vs documentation vs model-architecture disclosure).

**Berry anchor:** §5.1 (`sufficiently`, `appropriate`), §3.3.1 (`transparent` polysemy).

#### 2.13 AIA-C13 — Art. 13(3) Instructions for use

**Source locus:** `instructions for use in an appropriate digital format or otherwise that include concise, complete, correct and clear information that is relevant, accessible and comprehensible to deployers`.

* **VAG — S2** — `appropriate`, `concise`, `complete`, `correct`, `clear`, `relevant`, `accessible`, `comprehensible`. Eight vague adjectives in one sentence.
* **POLY — S2** — `characteristics, capabilities and limitations of performance` — three near-synonyms for system properties.
* **COORD — S2** — `concise, complete, correct AND clear` (4-way adjective AND); `relevant, accessible AND comprehensible` (3-way adjective AND). Two separate AND-chains compounding in one sentence.

**Berry anchor:** §5.1 (8 vague adjectives), §5.4.7 (twin AND-chains).

#### 2.20 AIA-C20 — Art. 17(1) Quality management system

**Source locus:** `Providers of high-risk AI systems shall put a quality management system in place that ensures compliance with this Regulation. That system shall be documented in a systematic and orderly manner in the form of written policies, procedures and instructions, and shall include at least the following aspects: (a) … (m)`.

* **VAG — S2** — `systematic and orderly manner`.
* **POLY — S2** — `quality management system` — ISO 9001 vs. ISO/IEC 23053 vs. internal bespoke.
* **COORD — S2** — `written policies, procedures AND instructions` (3-way AND); 13-element closed list (a)–(m) AND-coordinated.

**Berry anchor:** §5.1 (`systematic`, `orderly`), §3.3.1 (`QMS`), §5.4.7 (13-element list).

#### 2.22 AIA-C22 — Art. 17(1)(g) Risk management documentation

**Source locus:** `the risk management system referred to in Article 9` — single cross-reference constitutes the entire sub-point.

* **POLY — S2** — `risk management system` (inherits from C01).
* **COORD — S2** — implicit; the cross-reference defers to Art. 9 structure.

**Berry anchor:** §3.3.1 (`risk management system`).

#### 2.23 AIA-C23 — Art. 26(1) Deployer measures

**Source locus:** `Deployers of high-risk AI systems shall take appropriate technical and organisational measures to ensure they use such systems in accordance with the instructions for use`.

* **VAG — S2** — `appropriate`.
* **POLY — S2** — `appropriate` (legal hedge vs. technical recommendation).
* **COORD — S2** — `technical AND organisational` — same AND/OR pattern as GDPR Art. 5(1)(f) (C04); inclusive vs. exclusive reading material.

**Berry anchor:** §5.1 (`appropriate`), §5.4.7 (`technical and organisational`).

#### 2.24 AIA-C24 — Art. 26(2) Deployer oversight assignment

**Source locus:** `Deployers shall assign human oversight to natural persons who have the necessary competence, training and authority, as well as the necessary support`.

* **VAG — S2** — `necessary`.
* **POLY — S2** — `competence`, `training`, `authority` — three near-synonyms for personnel qualifications.
* **COORD — S2** — `necessary competence, training AND authority` (3-way AND); `as well as the necessary support` adds a fourth element.

**Berry anchor:** §5.1 (`necessary`), §3.3.1 (`competence`/`training`/`authority`), §5.4.7.
